Compute c, logsum, grad:
def c5_counts(h, n):
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full').astype(float)

def smooth_obj(h, alpha, n):
    dx = 2.0/n
    c = c5_counts(h,n)
    g = c*dx
    m = g.max()
    x = alpha*(g-m)
    # if alpha large, all underflow except max; set max ones
    ex = np.exp(x)
    z = ex.sum()
    if z <=0 or not finite:
        p = np.zeros_like(g); p[np.argmax(g)] = 1.0
        return m + 0.0, p, c
    p = ex / z
    val = m + np.log(z)/alpha
    return val, p, c

def smooth_jac(h, p, n):
    dx=2/n
    grad = np.zeros(n)
    # apply weights p to derivative of c counts
    # For d=0:
    w0 = p[n-1]
    if w0 !=0: grad += w0*(1 - 2*h)
    for d in range(1,n):
        wF = p[n-1-d]; wG = p[n-1+d]
        if wF == 0 and wG == 0: continue
        L = n-d
        a = h[:L]; b = h[d:]
        if wF !=0:
            grad[:L] += wF*(1 - b)
            grad[d:] += -wF*a
        if wG !=0:
            grad[d:] += wG*(1 - a)
            grad[:L] += -wG*b
    return dx * grad

Potential issue: smooth_jac loops over d for n=800 => 800 iterations; SLSQP jac maybe many, slow. We can accelerate gradient using convolution/pads. Let's derive a vectorized O(n) gradient from weights p.
For all d, contributions to grad[i] from F_d and G_d. For fixed i, which d affect? For F_d: if i < n-d (i in first), add wF*(1-h[i+d]); if i >= d (i in second), add wF*(-h[i-d]). So for each i, d ranges 1..n-1. We can compute via prefix sums of weighted products?
Let wF_d = p[n-1-d] dx? Actually grad without dx. Contribution from all F_d to grad[i]:
Term1: d=1..n-1-i (i+d < n) sum wF_d * (1 - h[i+d]) = sum wF_d - sum wF_d h[i+d].
Term2: d=1..i (i-d >=0) sum wF_d * (-h[i-d]).
So F contribution = sum_{d=1}^{n-1-i} wF_d - (convolution of wF with h) at indices? The h[i+d] with d shift; and h[i-d]. If define array wF_d for d>0. Then term2 = -∑_{d=1}^i wF_d h[i-d] = - (wF_conv_h)[i] where wF array at lags. Term1 first half = prefix_sums of wF from 1 to n-1-i. Similarly G contributions:
G: if i>=d: add wG_d*(1-h[i-d]) => ∑_{d=1}^i wG_d - ∑ wG_d h[i-d]; if i < n-d: add -wG_d h[i+d] => -∑_{d=1}^{n-1-i} wG_d h[i+d].
We can compute these O(n) using cumulative sums and convolutions. Since w arrays length n, convolution can be via np.convolve O(n^2) but n=400 fine; or FFT. But simpler loop might be okay. Bitset objective function can be optimized with vectorized? Let's implement qint_max(A,n,masks) returning qmax. Use while loop:
def qmax_int(A,n,masks):
    notA = full_mask ^ A
    qmax=0
    one = A
    for d in range(1,n):
        shifted = A >> d
        L = n-d
        mask = masks[L]
        # positive orientation: A_i=1, A_{i+d}=0
        q1 = (A & ~shifted & mask).bit_count()
        if q1 > qmax: qmax=q1
        # reverse orientation: A_{i+d}=1, A_i=0
        q2 = (shifted & notA & mask).bit_count()
        if q2 > qmax: qmax=q2
        # if qmax current maybe? no early
    return qmax
This computes both. We can precompute masks each run. Note ~(A>>d) in Python is infinite negative; use notA shifted? q1 = (A & (full_mask ^ (A>>d)) & mask)? But (A>>d) has bits i where A_{i+d}=1. For i≥L, bits 0; not needed. ~shifted includes all high bits 1; mask zeros them. So fine: q1 = (A & (~shifted) & mask).bit_count(). Use mask to limit.
Can early stop if qmax >= current? no.

For target feasibility:
def feasible_int(A, n, masks, Q):
    notA=full_mask^A
    for d in range(1,n):
       L=n-d; mask=masks[L]; shifted=A>>d
       if (A & ~shifted & mask).bit_count() > Q: return False
       if (shifted & notA & mask).bit_count() > Q: return False
    return True
This loops until fail. Good.

Feasibility/update:
```
def qmax_limited(A, n, masks, limit):
    notA = ((1 << n) - 1) ^ A  # pass fullmask
    qmax = 0
    for d in range(1,n):
        sh = A >> d
        L = n-d
        mask = masks[L]
        c1 = (A & (~sh) & mask).bit_count()
        if c1 > limit: return limit+1
        if c1 > qmax: qmax=c1
        c2 = (sh & notA & mask).bit_count()
        if c2 > limit: return limit+1
        if c2 > qmax: q2
    return qmax
```
For exact current, limit large 10**9. But `~sh` negative; mask needed. Good.

def active_lags(A,n,masks,full,Q):
  notA=full^A; act=[]
  for d... c1,c2; if c1==Q act append (d,1); if c2==Q act append(d,-1)
```

Golden section 1D convex:
```
def minimize_delta_phi(c, D, q1, q2, lo, hi):
    gr = (np.sqrt(5)-1)/2
    a=lo; b=hi
    c = a + (1-gr)*(b-a)
    d = a + gr*(b-a)
    fc = phi(c); fd=phi(d)
    for ...:
       if fc < fd: b=d; d=c; fd=fc; c=a+(1-gr)*(b-a); fc=phi(c)
       else: a=c; c=d; fc=fd; d=a+gr*(b-a); fd=phi(d)
    return (a+b)/2
```
No nested phi: implement `phi_delta(c, D, q1,q2, dx, delta)` top-level. But golden loop calls phi many times; top-level function call overhead. Could inline? def phi_delta(c,D,q1,q2,dx,delta):
    vals = c + delta*D
    m = np.max(vals)
    if q1 >=0:
       v = vals[q1] + delta*delta
       if v>m: m=v
    if q2...
    return m*dx
```

def minimize_phi(c, D, q1, q2, dx, lo, hi):
    gr = 0.6180339887498949
    a=lo; b=hi
    c1 = a + (1-gr)*(b-a)
    d1 = a + gr*(b-a)
    f1 = eval_phi(...)  # use helper top-level
    f2 = eval_phi(...)

       if f1 < f2:
          b=d1; d1=c1; f2=f1; c1=a+(1-gr)*(b-a); f1=eval_phi(c1)
       else:
          a=c1; c1=d1; f1=f2; d1=a+gr*(b-a); f2=eval_phi(d1)
    return 0.5*(a+b)
```

For mutual pair lags (F_d and G_d): product term -h_i h_j change has +δ² for each as above. What about if i and j are same? no. Other lags: If a lag includes i with k and j with k? Since a lag is set of pairs at a fixed distance; it can involve i multiple times? For a fixed d, i can be first in pair (i,i+d) and second in (i-d,i), so two terms both involving i and different partners. If j is also one of those partners? If distance d = |i-j|, then the lag has one mutual pair (i,j) maybe plus could have i as first with j and as second with j? Only one pair for given unordered pair. For d<|i-j| or >, i and j are not partners but could each interact separately with others; no cross quadratic between δ_i and δ_j because partners unchanged. But self terms only d0.     coeff[idxF] += -hprev

Let's design pair move function:
```
def try_pair_move(h,c,D?,i,j,n,current_c):
   a=h[i]; b=h[j]; lo=max(-a,b-1); hi=min(1-a,b); L=hi-lo
   if L<1e-12: return None
   d=abs(i-j); q0=n-1; q1=n-1-d if d>0 else -1; q2=n-1+d if d>0 else -1
   D = calc_coeff(i,h,n) - calc_coeff(j,h,n)
   # candidate relative t in list maybe 13 points: 0, 1/12,...,1, and 0.5
   best_delta=0; best_m=current_max_counts (c.max())
   # Include delta0=0? We need current m exactly to compare. c.max() maybe current.
   # coeffs list: 0, 1/8,1/4,3/8,1/2,5/8,3/4,7/8,1? This is 9. Maybe add 1/16,1/2+? 13.
   for t in cand:
       delta = lo + t*L
       m = eval_cand(c,D,q0,q1,q2,delta) # returns max count not dx
       if m < best_m - 1e-12: best...
       elif m < best_m: best...
   if best_delta not near 0 and best_m < current_m:
       update h and c using best_delta: h[i]+=delta; h[j]-=delta; c += delta*D; c[q0]-=2δ²; if q1 c[q1]+=δ²; q2...
       return best_m, delta
   else none
```
But choosing best from 9 candidates: if no candidate below current, skip. This monotone coordinate descent will avoid saddle? Could get stuck at grid local min even if fractional improvement not sampled. We can use smaller grid e.g., 17 candidate fractions (steps 1/16) to capture. 17 evals per pair; okay. We can also after best candidate, do local refinement by evaluating finer grid of 9 around best (neighbors in fraction). Implementation details for speed:
- Current_max_counts = np.max(c) (float). Use counts not C for comparisons; C=counts*dx. Use tolerance.
- `eval_candidate(c, D, q0, q1, q2, delta)` returns max count:
```
vals = c + delta*D
m = vals.max()
v = vals[q0] - 2.0*delta*delta
if v>m: m=v
if q1>=0:
   v = vals[q1] + delta*delta
   if v>m: m=v
   v = vals[q2] + delta*delta ...
return m
```
But performance: This allocates vals for each candidate. Could compute without allocation by using `np.dot`? Max of `c + delta*D`. Allocation maybe bottleneck. We can pre-compute `c` and `D` as Python lists? no.
Use NumPy vectorized: `m = np.maximum.reduce?` not.
Maybe compute candidates vector all at once? For all candidate deltas, matrix shape M x (2n-1) with D*delta + c; max over lags. M=17, length 800 => 13k, memory okay. Could evaluate all candidates in one go:
```
deltas = candidate vector length K
vals = c[None,:] + np.outer(deltas, D)  # M x Mlen
m = np.max(vals, axis=1)
# correct q0: m = np.maximum(m, vals[:,q0] - 2*deltas^2)
# q1/q2 similarly
```
This uses vectorized BLAS? `np.outer` allocation M*len. For 17*800=13k floats, small. For many pairs, allocation larger but maybe still okay. It may be faster than repeated Python loops over candidates. But local refinement adds. We can implement vectorized candidate eval function `eval_candidates(c,D,q1,q2,deltas)` returning max counts array.
```
def eval_candidates(c, D, q1, q2, deltas):
    # deltas 1D length K
    vals = c[None, :] + deltas[:, None] * D[None, :]
    m = np.max(vals, axis=1)
    dd = deltas*deltas
    v0 = vals[:, q0] - 2*dd; m=np.maximum(m,v0)
    if q1>=0: ...
    return m
```

```
def add_coeff(r, coeff, h, n, sgn):
   coeff[n-1] += sgn*(1-2h[r])
   if r+1<n:
      d = np.arange(1, n-r)
      idxG = n-1-d; idxF=n-1+d; hout=h[r+1:]
      coeff[idxG] += sgn*(-hout)
      coeff[idxF] += sgn*(1-hout)
   if r>0:
      d = np.arange(1,r+1)
      idxG=n-1-d; idxF=n-1+d; hprev=h[r-1::-1]
      coeff[idxG] += sgn*(1-hprev)
      coeff[idxF] += sgn*(-hprev)
```

Earlier gradient formulas with F/G swapped? Let's derive directly:
F_d (weight wF): ∂F/∂h_i for i<L: 1-h[i+d]; for i>=d: -h[i-d].
G_d (weight wG): G_d = ∑_{i=0}^{L-1} h[i+d](1-h[i]). ∂/∂h_i for i<L: -h[i+d]; for i>=d: 1-h[i-d].
Thus for each d:
```
L=n-d; a=h[:L]; b=h[d:]
if wF: grad[:L]+=wF*(1-b); grad[d:]+=-wF*a
if wG: grad[:L]+=-wG*b; grad[d:]+=wG*(1-a)
```

`eval_candidates` implementation:
```
def eval_candidates(c, D, q0, q1, q2, deltas):
    # deltas 1D
    vals = c[None, :] + deltas[:, None] * D[None, :]
    m = np.max(vals, axis=1)
    dd = deltas * deltas
    v = vals[:, q0] - 2.0*dd
    np.maximum(m, v, out=m)  # m already array. Use m = np.maximum(m, v)
    if q1 >= 0:
       v = vals[:, q1] + dd
       m = np.maximum(m, v)
       v = vals[:, q2] + dd
       m = np.maximum(m, v)
    return m
```
Memory okay. But `c[None,:] + deltas[:,None]*D[None,:]` uses broadcasting. We can optimize for n small.

m=base.max(axis=1)

...
```

def pair_search(h,c,n,deadline,seed,max_adjacent_sweeps, max_attempts, recompute_every=50):
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    cmax=c.max()
    dx=2/n
    success_count=0; attempts=0
    attempts_since_recomp=0
    # Adjacent sweeps maybe while time
    for sweep in range(max_adjacent_sweeps):
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       # alternate direction? We can loop i all
       for i in range(n-1):
          if time.time()>deadline: break
          attempts+=1
          cmax2 = try_pair_move_inplace(...)
          if cmax2 is not None and cmax2 < cmax:
              cmax=cmax2; success_count+=1; attempts_since_recomp+=1
          if cmax < best... but best tracked outside? We can keep local best_h? Pair_search returns h,c.
       if success_count==... maybe continue; if no improvement in sweep break?
    # random pairs
    # target number attempts by time
    while time.time()<deadline:
       # generate pair
       i=rng.randint(n); j=rng.randint(n); if i==j continue
       # maybe bias: if h[i]==h[j] unlikely move; could skip if both near same? Try anyway.
       attempts...

    return h,c,cmax
```

def fix_sum(h,n):

   # distribute to variable with room
   if diff>0:

A simple guided pair: For active lag d, consider positions i where the unnormalized product for that lag is high? The lag count c_d = ∑ h_i(1-h_j) for orientation. Now SLSQP objective gradient uses correct layout. Let's implement smooth functions.

`smooth_value_and_weights(h, alpha, n)`:
```
c = np.correlate(h,1-h).astype(float)
g = c * (2/n)
m = g.max()
x = alpha*(g-m)
ex = np.exp(x)
z = ex.sum()
if not finite or z==0: p=zeros; p[np.argmax(g)]=1
else p=ex/z
val = m + np.log(z)/alpha
return val,p,c
```
High alpha large x negative; okay. For alpha maybe >1e6, x for m 0, for others large negative; exp 0. z maybe 1. Good.

`smooth_gradient(h, p, n)`:
```
dx=2/n; grad=zeros(n); w0=p[n-1]
if w0>1e-15: grad += w0*(1-2h)
for d in range(1,n):
   wF=p[n-1+d]; wG=p[n-1-d]
   if wF < 1e-14 and wG <1e-14: continue
   L=n-d; a=h[:L]; b=h[d:]
   if wF >=1e-14:
      grad[:L] += wF*(1-b)
      grad[d:] += -wF*a
   if wG >=1e-14:
      grad[:L] += -wG*b
      grad[d:] += wG*(1-a)
return grad*dx
```
This gradient corresponds to f=logsumexp(alpha*g)/alpha? We used p as softmax of αg; derivative of f = ∑p ∂g. Yes.

SLSQP wrapper:
```
def slsqp_obj(x, alpha, n):
   val,p,c = smooth_value_and_weights(x, alpha, n)
   if not np.isfinite(val): return 1e6
   return val
def slsqp_jac(x, alpha, n):
   val,p,c = smooth_value_and_weights(x, alpha, n)
   return smooth_gradient(x,p,n)
def slsqp_eq(x,n): return x.sum() - n/2.0
```
Minimize call:
```
res = minimize(slsqp_obj, h, method='SLSQP', jac=slsqp_jac, args=(alpha,n), bounds=[(0,1)]*n, constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':slsqp_eq, 'args':(n,)}], options={'maxiter':maxit,'ftol':1e-16, 'iprint':0})
```
No lambdas.
Potential issue: SLSQP may modify h returned res.x not exactly in bounds; project. It also may not satisfy eq; project.

Need `project_box_sum` robust and not too slow. Implement top-level:
```
def project_box_sum(x, n):
    target = 0.5*n
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float).copy()
    # clip initial
    x = np.clip(x,0,1)
    # If sum close, fix small residual
    diff = target - x.sum()
    if abs(diff) <= 1e-12:
       return fix_sum(x,n)
    # Bisection for tau
    lo = float(x.min()) - 1.0
    hi = float(x.max()) + 1.0
    # ensure s(lo)>=target, s(hi)<=target
    for _ in range(20):
       s_lo = np.sum(np.clip(x - lo,0,1))
       if s_lo >= target: break
       lo -= 2.0
    for _ in range(20):
       s_hi = np.sum(np.clip(x - hi,0,1))
       if s_hi <= target: break
       hi += 2.0
    for _ in range(60):
       mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
       s_mid = np.sum(np.clip(x - mid,0,1))
       if s_mid > target: lo = mid
       else: hi = mid
    res = np.clip(x - 0.5*(lo+hi),0,1)
    return fix_sum(res,n)
```
But bisection `lo` should have s_lo >= target (sum high), `hi` s_hi <= target. If s_mid > target, need increase tau -> lo=mid? Since higher tau lower sum. We want find tau with s=target. If sum too high, tau too low, set lo=mid (raise lower bound). If sum too low, hi=mid. Good.
`fix_sum`:
```
def fix_sum(h,n):
  target=n/2.0
  diff=target-h.sum()
  if abs(diff)>1e-10:
     if diff>0:
        idx = np.argmin(h)
        # add while capacity
        need=diff
        while need>1e-14:
           room=1-h[idx]
           if room<=0:
              idx=(idx+1)%n
              continue
           add = min(room, need)
           h[idx]+=add; need-=add
           if need>1e-15: idx=np.argmin(h >?); # simpler use argsort room once
     else similar
  # final one adjust
  diff=target-h.sum()
  if abs(diff)>1e-14:
     mask=(h>1e-12)&(h<1-1e-12)
     if mask.sum()>0:
       idx=np.where(mask)[0][0]
       h[idx]+=diff
       h=np.clip(h,0,1)
  return h
```
Simpler: Use `np.argsort` for distribution:
```
def fix_sum(h,n):
 target=0.5*n; diff=target-h.sum()
 if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
   if diff>0:
     order=np.argsort(1.0-h)[::-1]
     need=diff
     for idx in order:
       if need<=1e-14: break
       room=1.0-h[idx]
       if room<=0: continue
       add = room if room<need else need
       h[idx]+=add; need-=add
   else:
     order=np.argsort(h)[::-1]
     need=-diff
     for idx in order:
       room=h[idx]
       sub=min(room,need); h[idx]-=sub; need-=sub
 # final exact if possible
 diff=target-h.sum()
 if abs(diff)>1e-13:
   mask=(h>1e-12)&(h<1-1e-12)
   if np.any(mask):
      idx=int(np.where(mask)[0][0])
      h[idx]+=diff
      h=np.clip(h,0,1)
 return h
```

def try_pair_move(h, c, i, j, n, cmax_counts):
    a = h[i]; b = h[j]
    lo = max(-a, b-1.0); hi = min(1.0-a, b)
    span = hi-lo
    if span <= 1e-13: return False, cmax_counts
    d = abs(i-j)
    q0 = n-1; q1 = n-1-d if d>0 else -1; q2 = n-1+d if d>0 else -1
    D = np.zeros(2*n-1)
    add_coeff(D, i, h, n, 1.0)
    add_coeff(D, j, h, n, -1.0)
    # if all D zero and d==0? d>0 if i!=j; quads maybe.
    deltas = np.linspace(lo, hi, 9)
    mvals = eval_candidates(c, D, q0, q1, q2, deltas)
    best_m = cmax_counts; best_t_rel = 0.0? Need best delta.
    # compare
    idx = np.argmin(mvals)
    if mvals[idx] < best_m - 1e-12:
       best_m = mvals[idx]; best_delta = deltas[idx]
    # local refine
    if best_delta !=0 and (span > 0):
       step = span/8.0
       # center delta
       if best_delta < lo + step:
          wlo=lo; whi=lo+3*step
       elif best_delta > hi - step:
          wlo=hi-3*step; whi=hi
       else:
          wlo=best_delta-2*step; whi=best_delta+2*step
          if wlo<lo: wlo=lo; whi=wlo+?; simpler clamp
       refine_deltas=np.linspace(max(lo,wlo), min(hi,whi), 9)
       rm=eval_candidates(...)
       jidx=...

    # if best_delta 0? no move
    if best_delta ==0: return False,cmax_counts
    # apply
    old_i, old_j = a,b
    h[i] = a + best_delta; h[j]=b - best_delta
    # clip within tol
    if h[i] < 0: h[i]=0
    elif h[i] > 1: h[i]=1

    # c may have tiny imaginary? no

Let's write `add_index_coeff` carefully to avoid temporary full arrays? It uses h slices.
```
def add_index_coeff(coeff, r, h, n, sgn):
    coeff[n-1] += sgn * (1.0 - 2.0*h[r])
    if r + 1 < n:
        d = np.arange(1, n - r)
        idxG = n - 1 - d
        idxF = n - 1 + d
        hout = h[r+1:]
        coeff[idxG] += -sgn * hout
        coeff[idxF] += sgn * (1.0 - hout)
    if r > 0:
        d = np.arange(1, r + 1)
        idxG = n - 1 - d
        idxF = n - 1 + d
        hprev = h[r-1::-1]
        coeff[idxG] += sgn * (1.0 - hprev)
        coeff[idxF] += -sgn * hprev
```
Check for G/F signs with correct layout:
For r as first (plus hout): G idx += -h, F idx += 1-h. Multiply sgn. So `coeff[idxG] += sgn*(-hout)`, `coeff[idxF] += sgn*(1-hout)`. Good.
For r as second (hprev): G idx += 1-hprev, F idx += -hprev. Good.

For n=2 earlier D computed correct.

Now, we should ensure `add_index_coeff` with `coeff[idxG] += sgn*(-hout)` where idxG array int, hout float; advanced indexing with repeated? no. But if idxG length large, it creates a temporary copy and adds back; fine.

Potential issue: If r middle, idxG plus and minus arrays overlap; they are separate operations, so accumulates. Good.

Now pair_search driver in detail with no closures. We'll pass time deadline.
```
def pair_search(h, c, n, deadline, seed, max_sweeps=10, max_attempts=5000):
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    dx = 2.0/n
    recomps = 0
    small_lags = [1,2,3,4,5]
    for lag in small_lags:
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        for i in range(n-lag):
            if time.time() > deadline: break
            j = i+lag
            succ, cmax2, c2 = try_pair_move(h,c,i,j,n,cmax)
            if succ:
                c = c2; cmax = cmax2
            else:
                c = c2
        # optional recompute after sweep
    # Active random
    attempts=0
    lag_values_time = time.time()
    lagw = build_lag_weights(c,n)
    while time.time() < deadline:
       # maybe rebuild weights every 200 attempts or on improvement? simpler every 200
       if attempts % 200 == 0:
           lagw = build_lag_weights(c,n)
       if rng.random() < 0.8:
           if lagw.sum() <=0: d=1
           else: d=int(rng.choice(np.arange(1,n), p=lagw/lagw.sum()))
           i=int(rng.randint(n-d)); j=i+d
       else:
           i=int(rng.randint(n)); j=int(rng.randint(n))
       if i==j: continue
       succ, cmax2, c2 = try_pair_move(...)
       if succ:
          c=c2; cmax=cmax2; attempts+=1
          if attempts % 200 == 0:

       else:
          c = c2
       attempts +=1
       # maybe cap max_attempts
       if attempts > max_attempts: break
    return h,c,cmax
```

def project_box_sum(x,n):
    x=np.clip(np.asarray(x,float),0,1)
    return fix_sum(x,n)

`fix_sum` as above. Ensure if diff large positive and all h=1? impossible if sum below target. It will set enough.

```
def fix_sum(h,n):
    target = 0.5*n
    h = np.asarray(h,float)
    h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
        if diff > 0:
            order = np.argsort(1.0-h)[::-1]
            need=diff
            for idx in order:
                if need <= 1e-14: break
                room = 1.0 - h[idx]
                if room <= 0: continue
                add = room if room < need else need
                h[idx] += add
                need -= add
        else:
            order = np.argsort(h)[::-1]
            need=-diff
            for idx in order:
                if need <= 1e-14: break
                add = h[idx] if h[idx] < need else need
                h[idx] -= add
                need -= add
    # final adjust one free
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-13:
       free = (h > 1e-12) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-12)
       if np.any(free):
           idx = int(np.where(free)[0][0])
           h[idx] += diff
           h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    return h
```
If after final clipping sum changes tiny; okay. For exact constraint maybe enough. We could repeat final few times? Not.

def compute_c5(h,n):
    c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')
    return float(np.max(c)*2.0/n)

def resample_h(h,n_old,n_new):
    if n_new == n_old: return h.copy()
    xo=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_old,endpoint=False)
    xn=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_new,endpoint=False)
    return project_box_sum(np.interp(xn,xo,h),n_new)
```

def build_lag_weights(c,n):

       G=c[n-2:0:-1]
       F=c[n:2*n-1]
       w[1:]=np.maximum(G,F)
    return w

`eval_candidates`:
```
def eval_candidates(c, D, q0, q1, q2, deltas):
    vals = c[None, :] + deltas[:, None] * D[None, :]
    m = np.max(vals, axis=1)
    dd = deltas * deltas
    v = vals[:, q0] - 2.0 * dd
    m = np.maximum(m, v)
    if q1 >= 0:
        v = vals[:, q1] + dd
        m = np.maximum(m, v)
        v = vals[:, q2] + dd
        m = np.maximum(m, v)
    return m
```
For q0 if q0 within c length. Good.

Potential overflow? no.

`add_index_coeff`: use `np.arange`; for n small. We can precompute? skip.
```
def add_index_coeff(coeff, r, h, n, sgn):
    cr = h[r]
    coeff[n-1] += sgn * (1.0 - 2.0 * cr)
    m = n - r - 1
    if m > 0:
        d = np.arange(1, m + 1)
        idxG = n - 1 - d
        idxF = n - 1 + d
        hout = h[r+1:r+m+1]
        coeff[idxG] += -sgn * hout
        coeff[idxF] += sgn * (1.0 - hout)
    m = r
    if m > 0:
        d = np.arange(1, m + 1)
        idxG = n - 1 - d
        idxF = n - 1 + d
        hprev = h[r-1:-1:-1]  # h[r-1]..h[0] length r
        coeff[idxG] += sgn * (1.0 - hprev)
        coeff[idxF] += -sgn * hprev
```
Check slice `h[r-1:-1:-1]`: If r=3, h[2:-1:-1] -> index2 only, not h1,h0. Need `h[r-1::-1]` stops at 0 inclusive. Use that. Length r. Good.
`h[r+1:]` length n-r-1.

`try_pair_move` code:
```
def try_pair_move(h,c,i,j,n,cmax):
    a=h[i]; b=h[j]
    lo = -a if -a > b-1.0 else b-1.0
    hi = 1.0-a if 1.0-a < b else b
    span = hi - lo
    if span <= 1e-13 or i == j:
        return False, cmax, c
    d = j - i
    if d < 0: d = -d
    q0 = n-1
    q1 = n-1-d if d > 0 else -1
    q2 = n-1+d if d > 0 else -1
    D = np.zeros(2*n - 1)
    add_index_coeff(D, i, h, n, 1.0)
    add_index_coeff(D, j, h, n, -1.0)
    best_m = cmax
    best_delta = 0.0
    deltas = np.linspace(lo, hi, 9)
    vals = eval_candidates(c, D, q0, q1, q2, deltas)
    idx = int(np.argmin(vals))
    if vals[idx] < best_m - 1e-12:
        best_m = float(vals[idx]); best_delta = float(deltas[idx])
    if abs(best_delta) > 1e-12:
        step = span / 8.0
        if best_delta < lo + step:
            wlo = lo; whi = lo + 3.0*step
        elif best_delta > hi - step:
            wlo = hi - 3.0*step; whi = hi
        else:
            wlo = best_delta - 2.0*step
            whi = best_delta + 2.0*step
            if wlo < lo:
                wlo = lo; whi = min(hi, wlo + 4.0*step)
            if whi > hi:
                whi = hi; wlo = max(lo, whi - 4.0*step)
        if whi - wlo > 1e-13:
            deltas2 = np.linspace(wlo, whi, 9)
            vals2 = eval_candidates(c, D, q0, q1, q2, deltas2)
            idx2 = int(np.argmin(vals2))
            if vals2[idx2] < best_m - 1e-12:
                best_m = float(vals2[idx2]); best_delta = float(deltas2[idx2])
    if abs(best_delta) <= 1e-12:
        return False, cmax, c
    old_hi = a; old_hj = b
    h[i] = a + best_delta
    h[j] = b - best_delta
    if h[i] < 0.0: h[i] = 0.0
    elif h[i] > 1.0: h[i] = 1.0
    if h[j] < 0.0: h[j] = 0.0
    elif h[j] > 1.0: h[j] = 1.0
    c_new = c + best_delta * D
    dd = best_delta * best_delta
    c_new[q0] -= 2.0 * dd
    if q1 >= 0:
        c_new[q1] += dd
        c_new[q2] += dd
    newmax = float(np.max(c_new))
    if newmax > cmax + 1e-8:
        h[i] = old_hi; h[j] = old_hj
        return False, cmax, c
    return True, newmax, c_new
```
If h clipping changes sum slightly, c update maybe slightly inconsistent. Clipping only if at bounds; if due rounding at lo/hi, maybe. To maintain sum exactly, if clip changed, adjust other variable? E.g., if h[i] clipped to 1, h[j] should be b - (1-a) instead of b-best_delta. We can set delta2 = h[i] - a after clipping, then h[j]=b-delta2? But best_delta chosen boundary, no clip needed. Due rounding, small. If clip occurs, we can recompute c from h after update instead of using D. Simpler: after clipping, if abs((a+h_i_after)- (b+h_j_after)) changes? We can just recompute c_new = correlate(h,1-h) if clipped or if `abs(h.sum()-old_sum)>...`? Recompute sometimes okay. Maybe easier: after h update/clipping, always recompute c_new? That's O(n^2) per improvement, too heavy if many. But improvements not too many? Could be thousands; 160k*1000=160M, maybe okay. But we wanted incremental. Clipping rare. We can check:
```
delta_eff = h[i] - a
if abs(delta_eff - best_delta) > 1e-14:
    # adjust h[j] to preserve sum: h[j] = b - delta_eff
    h[j] = b - delta_eff
    # recompute c_new
    c_new = np.correlate(h,1-h).astype(float)
else:
    c_new = c + best_delta*D ... quads
```
Good.

But if best_delta exactly lo/hi, due floating, h may be 0 or1. delta_eff slight. We'll recompute if diff >1e-12.

`pair_search`: Need recompute after some attempts. We'll pass `max_attempts` based on n/time. Maybe set `max_attempts = max(2000, int(8.0 * n))`? For n=400, 3200; plus small sweeps. We can let time control.
```
def pair_search(h,c,n,deadline,seed):
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    cmax=float(np.max(c))
    # small sweeps
    small = (1,2,3,4,5) if n>10 else (1,2)
    for lag in small:
       if time.time() > deadline: break
       for i in range(n-lag):
           if time.time() > deadline: break
           succ, cmax, c = try_pair_move(h,c,i,i+lag,n,cmax)
           if not succ and c is not c? no
    attempts=0; lagw=build_lag_weights(c,n); lagsum=lagw[1:].sum()
    while time.time() < deadline:
       if attempts % 200 == 0:
          lagw=build_lag_weights(c,n); lagsum=lagw[1:].sum()
       r=rng.random()
       if r < 0.8 and lagsum >0:
           p=lagw[1:]/lagsum
           d=int(rng.choice(n-1,p=p))+1
           i=int(rng.randint(n-d)); j=i+d
       elif r < 0.9:
           i=int(rng.randint(n)); j=int(rng.randint(n))
       else:
           j=i+1? maybe adjacent random
       if i==j: continue
       succ,cmax,c=try_pair_move(...)
       attempts +=1
    # final recompute
    c,cmax = recompute_c(h,n)
    return h,c,cmax
```
If succ false but c maybe recomputed rare; we assign every time. But `try_pair_move` returns original c on failure. Good.
Add a cap to avoid running forever if deadline huge? Time while handles.
Potential infinite if `try_pair_move` very fast and deadline far; but budget limited. Good.
Need small sweep over lag 1..5 all i. For n=400, 2000 attempts. Good.

`polish_slsqp`: Need alphas and maxiter. To avoid nested, use top-level.
```
def polish_slsqp(h,n,deadline,alphas,maxiter):
    cur=project_box_sum(h,n).copy()
    cur_c=compute_c5(cur,n)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':slsqp_eq,'args':(n,)}]
    for alpha in alphas:
       if time.time() > deadline - 3: break
       try:
          res=minimize(slsp_obj, cur, method='SLSQP', jac=slsqp_jac, args=(alpha,n), bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-16,'iprint':0})
          nh=project_box_sum(res.x,n)
          cval=compute_c5(nh,n)
          if cval < cur_c:
             cur=nh; cur_c=cval
       except Exception:
          break
    return cur,cur_c
```
If res.x None, break. Use `res.x` if success? If SLSQP raises, catch.

    return float(val)

`smooth_value_and_weights`: To avoid recompute c if caller needs? no.
```
def smooth_value_and_weights(h, alpha, n):
    c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full').astype(float)
    g=c*(2.0/n)
    m=float(np.max(g))
    x=alpha*(g-m)
    ex=np.exp(x)
    z=float(np.sum(ex))
    if not np.isfinite(z) or z <= 0.0:
       p=np.zeros_like(g)
       p[int(np.argmax(g))]=1.0
    else:
       p=ex/z
    val=m + np.log(z)/alpha if alpha>0 else m
    return val,p,c
```
Alpha always >0.

`smooth_gradient`: Maybe optimize by threshold p. Use `if p[t] > 1e-13`. Loop.
```
def smooth_gradient(h,p,n):
    dx=2.0/n
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    w=p[n-1]
    if w>1e-13: grad += w*(1.0-2.0*h)
    for d in range(1,n):
       wF=p[n-1+d]; wG=p[n-1-d]
       if wF < 1e-14 and wG < 1e-14: continue
       L=n-d
       a=h[:L]; b=h[d:]
       if wF > 1e-14:
          grad[:L] += wF*(1.0-b)
          grad[d:] += -wF*a
       if wG > 1e-14:
          grad[:L] += -wG*b
          grad[d:] += wG*(1.0-a)
    return dx*grad
```
This loops d. For n=600, okay.

Could SLSQP with high alpha fail due p all zeros? If alpha large and g-m negative huge, exp underflow all zero except max -> ex sum fine. If all inf? g-m <=0, exp <=1. okay.

```
def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100,**kwargs):
    t0=time.time()
    total = budget_s - 8
    try:
       h = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
       n = len(h)
    except Exception:
       n = 400
       h = np.ones(n)*0.5
    h = project_box_sum(h,n)
    c,cmax = recompute_c(h,n)
    dx=2.0/n
    best_c = cmax*dx
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_n = n
    # Phase 1 SLSQP quick
    p1 = t0 + 0.13*total
    if p1 > t0+5:
       h2,c2 = recompute... no
       h_s, cs = polish_slsqp(h,n,p1,[200,2000,20000],60)
       if cs < best_c: best...
       h = h_s.copy()
       c,cmax = recompute_c(h,n)
    dl = t0+total
    # set p2
    p2 = t0 + 0.55*total
    h_p, c_p, cmax_p = pair_search(h,c,n,p2,seed)
    c_ax = cmax_p*2.0/n; if <best update; h = h_p.copy(); c=c_p; cmax=cmax_p
    # Ensure feasibility
    h = project_box_sum(h,n); c,cmax = recompute_c(h,n); update
    p3 = t0 + 0.78*total
    h_s, cs = polish_slsqp(h,n,p3,[20000,200000,2000000],80)
    update; h=h_s.copy(); c,cmax=recompute
    p4 = t0 + 0.92*total
    h_p, c_p, cmax_p = pair_search(h,c,n,p4,seed+101)
    update
    # high-res if time and best above target
    if time.time() < dl - 120 and best_n < 600 and best_c > 0.38082:
       n2 = 600 if best_n < 600 else best_n*2? but keep <=800
       n2 = 600
       hr = resample_h(best_h,best_n,n2)
       # short pair + slsqp
       hr,cr,cmaxr = pair_search(hr, *recompute, deadline=t+ maybe 0.5*left, seed+202)
       # slsqp quick
       hr,csr = polish_slsqp(hr,n2, t0+left*0.3, [50000,500000],50)
       update if c < best; best_n=n2
    # final
    h=project_box_sum(best_h,best_n); c,cmax=recompute
    best_c = cmax*2.0/best_n
    return best_h, best_c, best_n
```

def fix_sum(h,n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    target = 0.5 * n
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
       ...
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-13:
       free = (h > 1e-12) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-12)
       if np.any(free):
          idx = int(np.where(free)[0][0])
          h[idx] += diff
          h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    return h

`pair_search`: At small sweeps, use local variables and recompute after each small lag? We can just loop. Need if `try_pair_move` reassigns c. Code:
```
def pair_search(...):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=float).copy()
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    for lag in (1,2,3,4,5):
       if lag >= n: break
       if time.time() > deadline: break
       for i in range(n-lag):
           if time.time() > deadline: break
           _, cmax, c = try_pair_move(h,c,i,i+lag,n,cmax)
    c,cmax = recompute_c(h,n)
    attempts=0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        if attempts % 100 == 0:
            c,cmax = recompute_c(h,n)
        lagw = build_lag_weights(c,n)
        lagsum = float(np.sum(lagw[1:]))
        if attempts % 100 == 0 or True? We recompute weights every 100; but above each loop would build weights every attempt if not careful.

```python
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time


def fix_sum(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    target = 0.5 * n
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
        if diff > 0.0:
            order = np.argsort(1.0 - h)[::-1]
            need = diff
            for idx in order:
                if need <= 1e-14:
                    break
                room = 1.0 - h[idx]
                if room <= 0.0:
                    continue
                add = room if room < need else need
                h[idx] += add
                need -= add
        else:
            order = np.argsort(h)[::-1]
            need = -diff
            for idx in order:
                if need <= 1e-14:
                    break
                room = h[idx]
                if room <= 0.0:
                    continue
                rem = room if room < need else need
                h[idx] -= rem
                need -= rem
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-13:
        free = (h > 1e-12) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-12)
        if np.any(free):
            idx = int(np.where(free)[0][0])
            h[idx] += diff
            h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    return h


def project_box_sum(h, n):
    return fix_sum(h, n)


def recompute_c(h, n):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full").astype(float)
    return c, float(np.max(c))


def compute_c5(h, n):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return float(np.max(c) * (2.0 / n))


def resample_h(h, n_old, n_new):
    if n_old == n_new:
        return h.copy()
    xo = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_old, endpoint=False)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_new, endpoint=False)
    return project_box_sum(np.interp(xn, xo, h), n_new)


def build_lag_weights(c, n):
    w = np.zeros(n)
    if n > 1:
        g = c[n - 2::-1]
        f = c[n:2 * n - 1]
        w[1:] = np.maximum(g, f)
    return w


def eval_candidates(c, D, q0, q1, q2, deltas):
    vals = c[None, :] + deltas[:, None] * D[None, :]
    m = np.max(vals, axis=1)
    dd = deltas * deltas
    v = vals[:, q0] - 2.0 * dd
    m = np.maximum(m, v)
    if q1 >= 0:
        v = vals[:, q1] + dd
        m = np.maximum(m, v)
        v = vals[:, q2] + dd
        m = np.maximum(m, v)
    return m


def add_index_coeff(coeff, r, h, n, sgn):
    coeff[n - 1] += sgn * (1.0 - 2.0 * h[r])
    m = n - r - 1
    if m > 0:
        d = np.arange(1, m + 1)
        idxg = n - 1 - d
        idxf = n - 1 + d
        hout = h[r + 1:r + m + 1]
        coeff[idxg] += -sgn * hout
        coeff[idxf] += sgn * (1.0 - hout)
    m = r
    if m > 0:
        d = np.arange(1, m + 1)
        idxg = n - 1 - d
        idxf = n - 1 + d
        hprev = h[r - 1::-1]
        coeff[idxg] += sgn * (1.0 - hprev)
        coeff[idxf] += -sgn * hprev


def try_pair_move(h, c, i, j, n, cmax):
    if i == j:
        return False, cmax, c
    a = h[i]
    b = h[j]
    lo = max(-a, b - 1.0)
    hi = min(1.0 - a, b)
    span = hi - lo
    if span <= 1e-13:
        return False, cmax, c

    d = abs(i - j)
    q0 = n - 1
    q1 = n - 1 - d if d > 0 else -1
    q2 = n - 1 + d if d > 0 else -1

    D = np.zeros(2 * n - 1)
    add_index_coeff(D, i, h, n, 1.0)
    add_index_coeff(D, j, h, n, -1.0)

    best_m = cmax
    best_delta = 0.0

    deltas = np.linspace(lo, hi, 9)
    vals = eval_candidates(c, D, q0, q1, q2, deltas)
    k = int(np.argmin(vals))
    if vals[k] < best_m - 1e-12:
        best_m = float(vals[k])
        best_delta = float(deltas[k])

    if abs(best_delta) > 1e-12:
        step = span / 8.0
        wlo = best_delta - 2.0 * step
        whi = best_delta + 2.0 * step
        if wlo < lo:
            wlo = lo
            whi = min(hi, lo + 4.0 * step)
        elif whi > hi:
            whi = hi
            wlo = max(lo, hi - 4.0 * step)
        if whi - wlo > 1e-13:
            deltas2 = np.linspace(wlo, whi, 9)
            vals2 = eval_candidates(c, D, q0, q1, q2, deltas2)
            k2 = int(np.argmin(vals2))
            if vals2[k2] < best_m - 1e-12:
                best_m = float(vals2[k2])
                best_delta = float(deltas2[k2])

    if abs(best_delta) <= 1e-12:
        return False, cmax, c

    old_i = a
    old_j = b
    h[i] = a + best_delta
    h[j] = b - best_delta
    if h[i] < 0.0 or h[i] > 1.0 or h[j] < 0.0 or h[j] > 1.0:
        h[i] = np.clip(h[i], 0.0, 1.0)
        h[j] = np.clip(h[j], 0.0, 1.0)
        c_new = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full").astype(float)
    else:
        c_new = c + best_delta * D
        dd = best_delta * best_delta
        c_new[q0] -= 2.0 * dd
        if q1 >= 0:
            c_new[q1] += dd
            c_new[q2] += dd

    newmax = float(np.max(c_new))
    if newmax > cmax + 1e-8:
        h[i] = old_i
        h[j] = old_j
        return False, cmax, c
    return True, newmax, c_new


def pair_search(h, c, n, deadline, seed):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=float).copy()
    c, cmax = recompute_c(h, n)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    for lag in (1, 2, 3, 4, 5):
        if lag >= n:
            break
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        for i in range(n - lag):
            if time.time() > deadline:
                break
            _, cmax, c = try_pair_move(h, c, i, i + lag, n, cmax)

    c, cmax = recompute_c(h, n)
    attempts = 0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        if attempts % 100 == 0:
            c, cmax = recompute_c(h, n)
            lagw = build_lag_weights(c, n)
            lagsum = float(np.sum(lagw[1:]))

        r = rng.random()
        if r < 0.8 and lagsum > 1e-300:
            p = lagw[1:] / lagsum
            d = int(rng.choice(n - 1, p=p)) + 1
            i = int(rng.randint(n - d))
            j = i + d
        elif r < 0.92:
            i = int(rng.randint(n))
            j = int(rng.randint(n))
        else:
            i = int(rng.randint(n))
            j = int(rng.randint(n))

        if i == j:
            continue

        _, cmax, c = try_pair_move(h, c, i, j, n, cmax)
        attempts += 1

    c, cmax = recompute_c(h, n)
    return h, c, cmax


def smooth_value_and_weights(h, alpha, n):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full").astype(float)
    g = c * (2.0 / n)
    m = float(np.max(g))
    x = alpha * (g - m)
    ex = np.exp(x)
    z = float(np.sum(ex))
    if not np.isfinite(z) or z <= 0.0:
        p = np.zeros_like(g)
        p[int(np.argmax(g))] = 1.0
    else:
        p = ex / z
    val = m + np.log(z) / alpha
    return val, p, c


def smooth_gradient(h, p, n):
    dx = 2.0 / n
    grad = np.zeros(n)
    w0 = p[n - 1]
    if w0 > 1e-13:
        grad += w0 * (1.0 - 2.0 * h)
    for d in range(1, n):
        wf = p[n - 1 + d]
        wg = p[n - 1 - d]
        if wf < 1e-14 and wg < 1e-14:
            continue
        L = n - d
        a = h[:L]
        b = h[d:]
        if wf > 1e-14:
            grad[:L] += wf * (1.0 - b)
            grad[d:] += -wf * a
        if wg > 1e-14:
            grad[:L] += -wg * b
            grad[d:] += wg * (1.0 - a)
    return dx * grad


def slsqp_obj(x, alpha, n):
    val, _, _ = smooth_value_and_weights(x, alpha, n)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return 1e6
    return float(val)


def slsqp_jac(x, alpha, n):
    val, p, _ = smooth_value_and_weights(x, alpha, n)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return np.zeros(len(x))
    return smooth_gradient(x, p, n)


def slsqp_eq(x, n):
    return float(np.sum(x) - 0.5 * n)


def polish_slsqp(h, n, deadline, alphas, maxiter):
    cur = project_box_sum(h, n).copy()
    cur_c = compute_c5(cur, n)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    cons = [{"type": "eq", "fun": slsqp_eq, "args": (n,)}]
    for alpha in alphas:
        if time.time() > deadline - 2:
            break
        try:
            res = minimize(
                slsqp_obj,
                cur,
                method="SLSQP",
                jac=slsqp_jac,
                args=(alpha, n),
                bounds=bounds,
                constraints=cons,
                options={"maxiter": maxiter, "ftol": 1e-16, "iprint": 0},
            )
            if res.x is not None and len(res.x) == n:
                nh = project_box_sum(res.x, n)
                cv = compute_c5(nh, n)
                if np.isfinite(cv) and cv < cur_c:
                    cur = nh
                    cur_c = cv
        except Exception:
            break
    return cur, cur_c


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    total = max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 10.0)
    dl = t0 + total

    try:
        h = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n = len(h)
    except Exception:
        n = 400
        z = np.random.RandomState(seed).choice(n, size=n // 2, replace=False)
        h = np.zeros(n)
        h[z] = 1.0

    h = project_box_sum(h, n)
    c, cmax = recompute_c(h, n)
    cur_c = cmax * (2.0 / n)

    cur_h = h.copy()
    cur_n = n
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = cur_c
    best_n = n

    if n > 400:
        maxit1 = 25
        maxit2 = 40
    elif n > 300:
        maxit1 = 40
        maxit2 = 75
    else:
        maxit1 = 30
        maxit2 = 60

    p1 = t0 + 0.12 * total
    if time.time() < p1:
        hs, cs = polish_slsqp(cur_h, cur_n, p1, [200, 2000, 20000], maxit1)
        if np.isfinite(cs) and cs < best_c + 0.002:
            cur_h, cur_n, cur_c = hs, len(hs), cs
            if cs < best_c:
                best_h, best_c, best_n = hs.copy(), cs, len(hs)
        c, cmax = recompute_c(cur_h, cur_n)
        cur_c = cmax * (2.0 / cur_n)

    p2 = t0 + 0.55 * total
    hp, cp, cmaxp = pair_search(cur_h, c, cur_n, p2, seed)
    cpp = cmaxp * (2.0 / cur_n)
    if np.isfinite(cpp) and cpp < best_c + 0.002:
        cur_h, cur_n, cur_c = hp, cur_n, cpp
        if cpp < best_c:
            best_h, best_c, best_n = hp.copy(), cpp, cur_n
    c, cmax = recompute_c(cur_h, cur_n)
    cur_c = cmax * (2.0 / cur_n)

    p3 = t0 + 0.74 * total
    if time.time() < p3:
        hs, cs = polish_slsqp(cur_h, cur_n, p3, [20000, 200000, 2000000], maxit2)
        if np.isfinite(cs) and cs < best_c + 0.002:
            cur_h, cur_n, cur_c = hs, len(hs), cs
            if cs < best_c:
                best_h, best_c, best_n = hs.copy(), cs, len(hs)
        c, cmax = recompute_c(cur_h, cur_n)
        cur_c = cmax * (2.0 / cur_n)

    p4 = t0 + 0.86 * total
    hp, cp, cmaxp = pair_search(cur_h, c, cur_n, p4, seed + 101)
    cpp = cmaxp * (2.0 / cur_n)
    if np.isfinite(cpp) and cpp < best_c + 0.002:
        cur_h, cur_n, cur_c = hp, cur_n, cpp
        if cpp < best_c:
            best_h, best_c, best_n = hp.copy(), cpp, cur_n

    left = dl - time.time()
    if left > 170.0 and best_n < 600 and best_c > 0.38082:
        n2 = 600
        hr = resample_h(best_h, best_n, n2)
        cr, cmaxr = recompute_c(hr, n2)
        dl_pair = t0 + min(left * 0.5, left - 60)
        if dl_pair > time.time():
            hr, cr, cmaxr = pair_search(hr, cr, n2, dl_pair, seed + 202)
        dl_sls = t0 + left * 0.82
        if time.time() < dl_sls:
            hr, cvv = polish_slsqp(hr, n2, dl_sls, [50000, 500000], 35)
            cvv = compute_c5(hr, n2)
            if np.isfinite(cvv) and cvv < best_c:
                best_h, best_c, best_n = hr.copy(), cvv, n2

    best_h = project_box_sum(best_h, best_n)
    c, cmax = recompute_c(best_h, best_n)
    best_c = cmax * (2.0 / best_n)
    return best_h, best_c, best_n
```
